16:I[44692,["/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/08cykig6vtx_p.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/07qi2okp-.yp9.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0frujd.eyhz4o.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0n4z.7yu76je-.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/09-o.77-p0p36.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/1304s0wcp2kce.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0j1uy9t~zxcf0.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/14_5o-nbg8k_r.js"],"DailyView"]
17:T459,1. **Spectral Probing Analysis**: At checkpoints along real training trajectories, the momentum buffer is decomposed into singular directions, and the loss-optimal step size along each direction is estimated on held-out data. The spectral profile is found to be highly anisotropic and stable: a 'volatile head' at the 'Edge-of-Stability' supports only small step sizes, while a 'tolerant bulk' permits much larger steps.
2. **Unified Perspective**: SGD allocates step size proportional to singular values (opposite to landscape suggestions); Adam's coordinate-wise rescaling mitigates but doesn't solve the mismatch; Muon's whitening to a uniform scale reallocates step mass from the head to the bulk.
3. **SAMuon Algorithm**: To exploit the bulk's capacity for larger steps, Spectral-Aware Muon (SAMuon) is proposed. It imposes a static, head-anchored prior on the whitened update. The full SAMuon fits a log-rank-linear shape via randomized SVD; the simplified SAMuon-lite uses power iteration to pin the head and uniformly amplifies the bulk. Both add no persistent state and negligible computational overhead.18:T4dd,The authors define an exact conditional-marginal oracle model to isolate parallelization error from model approximation error. A deterministic adaptive policy chooses the next reveal set from the observed partial assignment. The selected variables are then sampled independently using their true conditional marginals rather than from their true joint conditional. Approximation error is measured by forward KL, which has a chain-rule decomposition aligned with the reveal process and corresponds to excess expected log loss. Serial depth is the minimum target-averaged number of rounds needed to meet a KL budget. The central proof aligns each round's KL contribution with the conditional total correlation of the variables revealed in that round. The identity applies to random and history-dependent reveal sets. The paper then turns the identity into upper and lower bounds for structured families: logarithmic exact sampling for Bernoulli walks via hierarchical bisection, logarithmic-in-length zero-error schedules for finite-order Markov chains with a factor depending on Markov order, linear expected depth for uniform random permutations at fixed error, and several binary-alphabet constructions with polylogarithmic to polynomial depth.19:T53b,The theoretical results give the main evidence. For the Bernoulli walk, hierarchical bisection achieves exact sampling in logarithmically many rounds, while contiguous left-to-right schedules require linearly many rounds to maintain fixed accuracy. For uniform random permutations, every fixed-error policy needs a linear number of expected rounds; under a hard round cap, the optimal error reduces exactly to an integer-composition problem, yielding fixed-round asymptotics, a finite-blocklength pairs-first phase, and a joint scaling frontier. Over a binary alphabet, uniform balanced binary strings have depth on the order of squared log sequence length at any fixed positive error, while independent one-hot blocks give matching square-root-depth lower bounds against arbitrary deterministic value-adaptive policies. Rectangular block constructions realize polynomial exponents up to one half. The empirical section is diagnostic: it evaluates generic schedules and deployed decoding rules on 542 sequences from WikiText and model-generated text across four domains using a single 0.5B model and one sampling temperature. A paired self-sampling study on 231 held-out prompts compares teacher-forced pseudo-costs with output quality under an external autoregressive evaluator, and also tests a model-independent spaced-profile schedule.1a:T5f7,The core mechanism is Expert Folding. Instead of dropping an excluded source expert or rerouting it to a similar target expert without correction, ExFold projects the source contribution onto a retained target expert. The method is motivated by two empirical findings: many experts produce directionally aligned outputs on the same inputs, but their output magnitudes differ substantially. Directional alignment makes substitution plausible; magnitude mismatch makes naive rerouting inaccurate. Based on this, each layer is calibrated with two matrices from a single frozen forward pass on unlabeled text. The scalar-projector matrix stores directed source-to-target scaling factors. The projection-loss matrix measures reconstruction loss for folding one expert into another. At inference time, retained experts are selected according to the phase budget: K_pre dominant experts per token for prefill, and D dominant experts per batch for decode. Every excluded expert is then assigned to the minimum-loss retained target, and its contribution is folded into that target by absorbing the scalar projector into the router weight. This gives one shared folding operator across both phases. Compared with token-wise sparsification, the method does not only reduce executed experts; compared with expert-set consolidation, it does not bake compression into a fixed submodel; compared with similarity-based rerouting, it explicitly corrects scale mismatch. The implementation is integrated into vLLM with a lightweight CUDA kernel.1b:T4a9,The evaluation covers multiple MoE architectures and real vLLM serving workloads, focusing on TTFT, TPOT, and quality retention. The headline numbers are up to 1.41× TTFT speedup and 2.45× TPOT speedup while preserving about 99% of the original average quality. The larger decode gain is consistent with the method’s focus on reducing the active expert set under memory-bound conditions. The more informative part is the comparison structure. Against pruning or skipping baselines, ExFold degrades quality more slowly at the same budget, which supports the claim that explicit reconstruction matters. Against static merging methods, it remains adaptive to token-level or batch-level budgets instead of committing to one permanently compressed model. Against similarity-based rerouting, the directed scalar projector improves approximation when experts are aligned in direction but not in scale. The ablations indicate that both components—the loss-based target assignment and the scalar correction—are necessary. Another useful result is that the same calibrated matrices work for both prefill and decode, which reduces deployment complexity and supports the phase-unified formulation.1c:T493,Hydra is a common-schema instrumentation and analysis framework. The authors instrument both HuggingFace Transformers and llama.cpp so that two very different inference stacks emit the same per-prompt timing record. The canonical schema covers at least tokenization, prefill, per-token generation, de-tokenization, inter-token latency, and end-to-end latency. These phase boundaries are then fused with high-resolution telemetry from tegrastats and NVML, enabling attribution of CPU activity, GPU utilization, DRAM traffic, power, energy, and thermal behavior to the prefill and decode windows. Two design choices matter. First, the measurement interface is decoupled from execution configuration, so models, precisions, prompt lengths, and SoC generations can be swapped without changing the analysis pipeline. Second, the framework spans both the Python framework path and the GGML/C++ path, which makes the comparison more realistic for deployment. The instantiated study covers three Jetson generations—AGX Xavier, AGX Orin, and AGX Thor—two backends, 13 instruction-tuned models from seven families, and five execution formats: bf16, F16, Q8_0, Q6_K, and Q4_K_M.1d:T46b,The paper is more about stable findings than about one best number. First, performance depends jointly on backend, architecture, precision, and sequence length; model size alone is not predictive enough. Hydra’s phase-level timing shows that backend overhead, quantized decode throughput, and input/output-length scaling affect different parts of the inference pipeline. Second, quantization often reduces DRAM traffic and model footprint, but it does not automatically reduce latency or energy on edge SoCs, because CPU orchestration, GPU effective utilization, shared-memory bandwidth, DVFS, and thermal limits also matter. Third, backend choice changes CPU-side runtime behavior enough to alter decode throughput and efficiency. Fourth, gains across SoC generations are not uniformly realized; newer hardware can improve throughput or energy efficiency in some settings, but backend overheads or thermal constraints can offset part of the benefit in others. A central empirical point is that phase-aligned telemetry is necessary: aggregate latency or run-averaged power hides meaningful differences between prefill and decode.1e:T78d,The method has four stages.

1) Direction estimation. For each layer, the model collects hidden states from reflective and non-reflective examples and computes a standard mean-difference vector as an initial reflection direction. This follows the usual activation-engineering assumption that a behavior can be approximated by a linear direction in residual space.

2) Direction purification. This is the key technical step. The authors argue that the raw contrast direction mixes reflection with activation structure shared by reasoning states more broadly. They therefore apply PCA-based denoising to reduce sample noise and low-value variation, then orthogonalize the candidate reflection direction against a rank-one proxy for shared reasoning structure. A central empirical claim is that the raw direction has substantial overlap with this shared proxy, while the orthogonalized direction reduces that overlap to roughly an isotropic-random baseline.

3) Layer calibration. The method does not assume every layer is a good intervention site. On a small calibration set, it sweeps candidate layers and intervention strengths, then keeps the layers that consistently reduce reflection-like behavior without destabilizing generation quality.

4) State-dependent projection attenuation. Instead of adding or subtracting a fixed vector at every token, the method removes only the component of the current hidden state that lies along the learned reflection direction, with bounded magnitude. This makes the intervention conditional on how much reflection-associated signal is present in the current token state. Compared with constant shifts, this is a more selective suppression mechanism and is less likely to damage ordinary reasoning.

The design choice is deliberate: no retraining, no controller network, no modified decoding. The trade-off is that it needs a small reflective/non-reflective contrast set and a calibration pass.1f:T6e1,The evaluation covers three open-weight Qwen-family checkpoints on two benchmarks, MATH-500 and GPQA-Diamond, with matched answer parsing and token accounting. The main comparison is against two closely related representation-level baselines, CREST and ReflCtrl.

Across six model-benchmark settings, Reflection Steering reduces reasoning tokens by 16.9% on average while largely preserving task performance. The cleanest result is on Qwen3-30B-A3B for MATH-500: a 21.8% token reduction with only a -0.1 point change in accuracy. The paper also reports paired equivalence testing and finds support for equivalence to the raw model within a ±1 point margin, which is stronger evidence than simply reporting a small average drop.

The paper’s own framing makes clear that the strongest accuracy-preserving evidence is on mathematical reasoning. On GPQA-Diamond, the token savings remain meaningful, but the trade-off is less clean. That makes sense: in math, reflection often appears as repeated verification after the solution path is already established, whereas in scientific reasoning, revision and reconsideration may be more tightly coupled to useful inference.

The ablations that matter are: (i) raw mean-difference direction versus purified direction, showing that orthogonalization against shared reasoning structure materially improves the accuracy-efficiency trade-off; (ii) fixed vector shifts versus projection attenuation, where the latter better preserves accuracy; and (iii) layer calibration, showing that intervention quality depends strongly on depth. This is not a paper about raising benchmark accuracy. It is about recovering tokens without paying much accuracy tax, and the right way to judge it is by the cleanliness of that trade-off.20:T6e6,The core method is residual-guided non-parametric optimization. The system starts from a fixed base verifier and iteratively improves it around observed mistakes. At each round, the current verifier is replayed on a construction pool, and disagreements with labels are collected as residuals. Recurring residual patterns are then abstracted into rule cards. Each card records a missing verifier bias together with support examples, counterexamples, applicability conditions, and abstention conditions. This is a key design choice: instead of directly updating model parameters, the method converts recurring failure modes into explicit knowledge units.

A rule card can follow one of two promotion paths. If the missing bias can be expressed as deterministic comparison logic—such as numerical tolerance handling, unit normalization, scientific notation conversion, or specific symbolic equivalence rules—it goes through code audit and replay validation, and if accepted becomes a code module. A code module is a deterministic verifier unit that returns Correct, Incorrect, or Abstain. If the bias cannot be fully codified and still requires model judgment, it is incorporated into the fallback verifier prompt rather than compiled into code. At inference time, the system first runs the module library; cases that can be decided explicitly are handled there, and only unresolved cases are sent to a fixed model-based fallback verifier.

What distinguishes this from prior work is not simply adding tools, but turning verifier improvement into a closed loop: residual discovery, rule-card drafting, replay validation, and promotion into either executable modules or prompt guidance. It sits between hand-maintained rule libraries and opaque judge-model behavior.21:T545,The paper evaluates on four reference-based verification benchmarks. The headline result is 93.05% macro accuracy, outperforming the reported rule-based, model-based, scientific, and tool-augmented baselines included in the study. The more informative result is the component ablation: compared with a prompt-only setting, adding code modules yields an additional +1.76 macro-accuracy gain. That suggests the benefit is not merely better prompting; explicit comparison logic is covering verifier blind spots that prompting alone does not fix. In addition, direct module decisions reduce fallback model calls by 32.13%, so the system improves both accuracy and inference efficiency.

The experiments are most convincing when read as evidence for two claims. First, recurring residuals do contain reusable verifier biases that can be extracted. Second, splitting those biases into deterministic modules versus prompt-level guidance works better than relying on a single prompt-only or model-only verifier. A careful reader should still pay attention to the relationship between the construction pool and the evaluation benchmarks: if they are too similar, rule extraction becomes easier. The replay-validation protocol, support/counterexample design, and cross-benchmark consistency are therefore important for judging how robust the method really is.22:T462,For a quick read, start with the motivating examples in the introduction, especially the discussion of when 1+3.14 should or should not be treated as equivalent to 1+π. That example captures the paper’s point well: the challenge is not only symbolic manipulation, but context-dependent grading assumptions. Then read the method section with four objects in mind: residuals, rule cards, code modules, and the fallback prompt. Once the transitions among these four are clear, the whole system becomes easy to follow.

In the experiments, prioritize the main comparison table and the ablations, especially prompt-only versus prompt-plus-code-modules, and the reduction in fallback calls. Around 20 minutes should be enough for a solid skim. If the goal is to assess whether this can plug into an RL reward pipeline, look more closely at replay-validation criteria, the abstain mechanism, and how much human effort is still needed to cluster or formalize recurring error patterns. Those details determine whether the method is a maintainable verifier framework or a semi-automatic workflow with significant manual overhead.23:T604,The core contribution is FrontierChallenge, a cross-domain benchmark of end-to-end scientific workflows. The full collection contains 300 tasks; 97 are released and evaluated in this work, while 203 remain held out internally. The released tasks span six domains: quantum chemistry, molecular dynamics, materials characterization, analytical chemistry, life science, and electrochemistry/environment.

Each task is packaged as a self-contained unit with aligned components including a task description, fixed inputs, an execution environment, a deliverable contract, and a task-specific grader. The curation follows four principles: representativeness, complexity, diversity, and verifiability. In practice, that means tasks must resemble real professional workflows, require dependency-aware multi-step execution, avoid trivial template variation, and support automated, repeatable scoring through files, numerical checks, plots, or explicit acceptance criteria.

The evaluation protocol is built around two metrics. Pass Rate is the primary metric and requires satisfying the full task contract. Avg. Score is secondary and measures partial progress under task-specific rubrics. This distinction is central to the paper: many agents can produce some correct intermediate outputs, but still fail the workflow because the final bundle is incomplete, inconsistent, or not reproducible. The authors evaluate 12 frontier models under 3 agent scaffolds and also analyze 970 Claude Code trajectories to characterize failure modes and end states.24:T5c7,The released benchmark contains 97 tasks, evaluated with 12 frontier models and 3 agent scaffolds. Tasks require real scientific software such as ORCA, CP2K, LAMMPS, AmberTools, and PLUMED, and demand executable code, quantitative analysis, visualizations, and evidence-grounded reports.

The main empirical result is a large gap between partial progress and full completion. The best-performing configurations—GPT-5.6 Sol with Codex and Grok 4.6 with Claude Code—complete only 20 of 97 tasks, corresponding to a Pass Rate of 20.6%. Yet the highest Avg. Score reaches 87.9. So the models often make substantial progress on substeps while still failing to satisfy the complete deliverable contract.

This gap is even sharper in some domains. In analytical chemistry, the highest Avg. Score is 87.6, but the best Pass Rate is only 4%. In electrochemistry/environment, the highest Avg. Score is 94.9, while the best Pass Rate is 0%. That pattern suggests the bottleneck is often not local scientific knowledge alone, but end-to-end execution reliability: file organization, consistency across outputs, formatting constraints, edge-case handling, and coherence among code, figures, tables, and prose.

The paper also includes failure analysis through contract-breach signatures and final-status/error behavior in 970 Claude Code trajectories. Even from the available text, the benchmark is clearly designed to stress workflow completion rather than isolated subskill performance.25:T415,This is a skim paper. Start with the introduction to understand the scope boundary: the benchmark is not about autonomous science or open-ended discovery, but about executing a specified workflow once the objective, inputs, and environment are fixed.

Then read the benchmark construction and evaluation protocol carefully, especially the task-package design, the distinction between Pass Rate and Avg. Score, and the rationale for using contract-level completion as the primary metric. That is where the benchmark gets its value.

Finally, inspect the main result tables and failure analysis. The two patterns worth tracking are the effect of different agent scaffolds and the domain-specific cases where Avg. Score is high but Pass Rate remains very low. A useful skim should take around 20-30 minutes.

From a foundation-model angle, the most useful questions are whether the observed failures are primarily model-capability failures or scaffold failures, and whether this benchmark can guide continual pretraining or tool-use data synthesis.26:T5dc,VBVR-Pro is a suite, not a single architecture. Its method has three main layers. First, task generation: the authors build 300 procedural tasks with randomized parameters, allowing scalable sample generation. Each task emits aligned outputs in multiple modalities, including videos, keyframe images, and interleaved text-visual representations. This is important because it lets image generators, video generators, and interleaved generators be trained and compared on the same underlying task distribution rather than on loosely matched datasets. Second, verifiable feedback: instead of relying on generic MLLM judges, the suite pairs tasks with structured semantic extraction and task-grounded verification scorers. Generated visual trajectories are parsed into structured semantics and then checked against task rules programmatically, producing rewards that can be used both for evaluation and reinforcement learning. The paper stresses that reward quality is validated at the instance level through arena-style human studies, not just through aggregate correlations. Third, optimization and substrate comparison: with these verifiable rewards, the authors run multi-task RL for visual reasoning and compare image, video, and interleaved generative substrates under controlled conditions. They also include diagnostics such as nearest-neighbor analysis and same-prompt visual counterfactuals to test whether gains come from reusable reasoning operations rather than instruction-template fitting.27:T664,The empirical story has three main parts. First is transfer. Models trained on VBVR-Pro reportedly gain consistently on seven external visual reasoning benchmarks, often by more than 20 percentage points. The named benchmarks include RISE-Video, V-ReasonBench, RULER-Bench, MME-CoF-Pro, VideoThinkBench, BabyVision, and IntelligentVBench. The authors use this to argue that broad coverage of reasoning mechanisms matters more than simply scaling the number of examples from a narrow task family. Second is reward quality. The paper analyzes failure modes of strong MLLM judges such as GPT-5.5, Qwen-3.7-plus, and Gemini-3.1-Pro, highlighting numerical imprecision, missed fine-grained evidence, and rule misunderstanding. In contrast, the task-grounded verifiers show stronger agreement with human judgments in instance-level arena studies and provide cleaner learning signals. Third is optimization. When these verifiable rewards are used for multi-task RL, performance improves steadily even from strong baselines, suggesting that the rewards are useful for training rather than only for offline scoring. The paper also includes anti-shortcut diagnostics: nearest-neighbor analysis suggests that external benchmark gains are not explained by close training-set matches, and same-prompt counterfactuals show sensitivity to changes in visual state rather than just textual prompts. The provided full-text excerpt does not include all detailed tables, so I cannot reproduce every model size and absolute score here, but the experimental emphasis is clearly on validating the suite as scalable, verifiable, and optimizable infrastructure.28:T422,Read it as an infrastructure paper, not as a new-model paper. Start with the introduction and keep the three threads in mind: task scaling, verifiable rewards, and substrate comparison. Then move to the task-construction section to understand how the 300 procedural tasks generate aligned image, video, and interleaved data, and why that alignment matters for fair comparison. After that, focus on the reward-design and judge-analysis sections. Those are the most transferable parts, especially the instance-level human evaluation protocol and the construction of the verifiers. Then read the transfer and RL sections to check whether the rewards actually support learning rather than just evaluation. If time is limited, prioritize task design, reward verification, and external transfer results; about 40 minutes should be enough for a solid pass. For deeper reading, the counterfactual and nearest-neighbor diagnostics are worth attention because they directly address whether the models learned reasoning operations or only matched superficial templates.29:T5d5,The core idea is a factorized benchmark design. Each jailbreak instance is decomposed along four axes: (1) harmful intent, with 272 intents; (2) prompt framing, with 6 templates controlling narrative structure and output requirements; (3) visual semantics, with 5 types of task-relevant visual context; and (4) instruction carrier, distinguishing direct textual instructions from visually rendered instructions. The benchmark then evaluates matched configurations where one factor changes while others are held fixed.

That matched-comparison setup is the key methodological choice. Instead of treating each prompt-image pair as an indivisible attack example, the benchmark treats the combinatorial factor space as the object of evaluation. This allows factor-level attribution rather than only aggregate jailbreak rates. The paper also provides a modular evaluation suite with full and lightweight settings, multiple judge options, and multidimensional metrics, aiming for reproducible and scalable auditing.

The paper further includes diagnostic analysis on a representative open-weight model, looking at internal representations and cross-modal interactions associated with vulnerability. That part is less about a new method and more about linking behavioral failures to representation-level patterns. Compared with prior benchmarks built from fixed multimodal attack instances, the main difference is that MMJailBench is structured for controlled causal-style comparisons across factors.2a:T478,The evaluation covers 16 open-weight and proprietary MLLMs. Four findings stand out. First, vulnerability is highly uneven across harm domains, with cyber abuse, economic harm, privacy violations, and deception/manipulation showing higher susceptibility. Second, prompt framing is the dominant source of variation: even with the same harmful intent, changing the narrative wrapper or requested output format can substantially alter refusal behavior. Third, task-relevant visual semantics systematically weaken refusal, and authority-like cues such as authorization documents or identity credentials are especially effective. Fourth, the effect of instruction carrier is strongly model-dependent; visually rendered instructions do not uniformly increase vulnerability.

The paper also discusses judge choices, full vs. lightweight configurations, and multidimensional metrics. The main empirical contribution is the heterogeneity and attribution structure, not a single leaderboard number.2b:T551,LifePlanner is built around (1) a realistic environment and (2) a structured evaluation protocol.
1) Environment: a ~10 km^2 urban region with ~3,600 deduplicated places (spatial anchors) built from AMap API + OpenStreetMap. Each anchor is enriched with weakly-structured local social media evidence from RedNote: up to 20 notes per place via name search, and up to 10 comments per note, retaining full text, timestamps, engagement stats, and tags—yielding ~200k notes/comments. Ambiguity and irrelevant content are intentionally preserved.
2) Attribute distillation for case construction: Gemini-2.5-Flash extracts static tags (facilities/offerings) and dynamic states (temporary closures/new arrivals). Crucially, these distilled “dynamic memory” fields are used to construct tasks but are hidden from evaluated agents to prevent leakage.
3) Access separation + MCP tools: during evaluation, agents must retrieve and interpret raw posts/comments through an MCP toolset, alongside map/routing utilities.
4) Evaluation protocol: 667 synthetic yet realistic queries across four task families (Place Perception, Nearby Discovery, Routing, Trip Design) and three difficulty tiers: L0 single retrieval; L1 multi-retrieval + calculation; L2 decision-making under additional user constraints. Agents output structured JSON for multi-aspect scoring and analysis.2c:T41f,The empirical audit is run on Inspect Evals at pinned commit 32b79a2. From 129 strict-path candidates, 5 are excluded by predeclared mechanical rules, leaving 124 eligible units. Every unit receives a terminal disposition. Of these, 110 stop before deterministic claim analysis because required historical evidence or semantic grounding is missing; 103 of these stops are from the outcome-blind census-review path and 7 from the frozen random draw. Only 14 units reach deterministic claim audit. The completed audits show that fine-grained and coarse-grained claims can diverge. In AgentDojo, five grounded readings change displayed scores. The primary family keeps Claude as the winner, yields a unique complete order, and stabilizes all three pairwise relations. In the wider review envelope, Claude remains the winner, but the lower two systems can swap, so the full order is not identified. Controlled interventions in Appendix L show that changing an omitted semantic coordinate can change the conclusion even with fixed observations and predictions.2d:T6cd,The method has two layers. First, a diagnostic framework. In each trajectory, the agent retrieves candidate pages, inspects page images, writes source-linked findings into working memory, and finally outputs both a final answer and terminal memory. A frozen reader then sees only the question plus terminal memory and produces a memory-only answer. An official judge scores both the final answer and the memory-only answer against the gold answer, yielding binary indicators for answer correctness and memory answerability. This produces a four-cell analysis, with special attention to P_mmc, the fraction of answer-correct cases whose terminal memory is still not answerable. The key design choice is that memory quality is defined functionally—whether it preserves enough information for answering—rather than stylistically.

Second, the training algorithm AWM-GRPO. All compared agent variants share the same Qwen3-VL-4B policy, retrieval harness, tools, memory schema, prompts, and optimizer; only post-training and reward differ. For each sampled trajectory, the policy emits a final answer and terminal memory; the frozen reader answers from memory alone; the judge scores both outputs. GRPO then ranks trajectories using not only final-answer correctness but also memory-only answerability, preferring trajectories that both solve the task and leave behind answerable memory. This differs from answer-only GRPO, which optimizes the end result but is indifferent to the quality of the stored state. It also differs from direct memory supervision: instead of imitating a gold summary, the method supervises memory indirectly through its ability to support answering, which is closer to the actual role memory plays in an agent loop.2e:T57a,The experimental setup is carefully controlled. The AWM-Agent variants use the same 4B backbone, the same RAG Top-3 retrieval harness, the same tools, and the same memory schema, so the observed differences are attributable to training signals rather than system changes. The paper first establishes the diagnostic gap: on a fixed 500-example answerable subset of MMLongBench-Doc, even under an evidence-page-given setting where gold evidence pages are supplied, 42.5% of correctly answered examples still fall into MMC. That is an important result because it shows the problem is not merely retrieval failure; it is failure to write sufficiently specific evidence into memory.

On end-task performance, AWM-GRPO outperforms RAG Top-3 by 8.1 points on MMLongBench-Doc and 11.9 on LongDocURL, beats SFT by 4.7 and 4.4 points, and improves over answer-only GRPO by 2.3 and 2.7 points. The paper also reports memory-quality diagnostics: on MMLongBench-Doc, AWM-GRPO reduces the memory-missing-correct rate by 2.7 points relative to answer-only GRPO. That matters because it shows the gain is not just a stronger answer policy; terminal memory itself becomes more usable. A controlled memory comparison further shows that replacing terminal memory with more complete, question-specific memory improves reader performance, which supports the causal motivation behind optimizing the memory artifact directly.2f:T460,For a quick pass, focus on three parts. First, read the introduction and the section defining memory-only answerability and MMC. That is where the paper’s real contribution sits: it adds a missing evaluation axis rather than merely proposing another agent. Second, inspect the AWM-GRPO training loop and pay attention to the division of labor between the frozen reader and the official judge. The important question is why the authors supervise memory through answerability instead of direct text matching. Third, read the diagnostic results, especially the evidence-page-given analysis and the four-cell breakdown, because those results justify the whole method.

If time is limited, a good order is Figure 1 and Table 1, then the diagnostic section, then the main results table, and finally the reward details. Around 20–30 minutes should be enough for the main idea. The deeper questions worth thinking about are whether reader/judge bias can distort the reward, and how easily this framework transfers to text-only agents, coding agents, or long-context summarizers where intermediate state quality also matters.30:T72d,LongVU-TTT inserts a TTT-Conv resampler between a pretrained ViT encoder and the LLM. The input to this stage is a per-frame spatial grid of visual features, preserving a 27x27 token layout instead of flattening everything into a 1D sequence upfront. The method has three main ingredients. First, the fast-weight parameterization is convolutional rather than MLP-based. Unlike TTT-MLP or SSM-style operators such as Mamba that process flattened sequences, the hidden state here is represented by two grouped convolution kernels, W1 and W2, which together define a time-varying convolutional mapping over the 2D feature grid. Grouping partitions channels into subsets, allowing specialization while keeping the update efficient. Second, the module uses causal inner-loop updates. For each frame, lightweight heads produce keys, values, queries, per-group learning-rate gates, and momentum coefficients. These signals drive gradient-style updates to the fast weights, and the updated convolutional mapping contextualizes the current stream using only preceding history. The causality claim is specifically about update ordering: each frame can only depend on accumulated past state, not future frames. Third, the model uses a hybrid frame selector. Instead of training an auxiliary scoring network, it derives change-aware importance directly from the inner-loop training signals, combining gradient-direction shifts and alignment-loss deltas with uniform sampling. This is meant to preserve both scene transitions and within-scene changes under a fixed frame budget. The full pipeline processes videos in chunks with state carried across chunk boundaries, normalizes importance after the full pass, selects a fixed number of frames, and then applies the original MLP projector to map selected visual features into the LLM embedding space.31:T5d6,The strongest part of the empirical section is the controlled comparison setup. The paper compares temporal Transformer, Gated DeltaNet, Mamba2, TTT-MLP, and TTT-Conv under the same baseline as much as possible, rather than mixing different backbones and training corpora. The headline numbers from the abstract are that TTT-Conv improves over TTT-MLP by up to 2.12 points on MLVU and over bidirectional Mamba2 by up to 3.04 points, while also outperforming attention-based and fixed-state recurrent resamplers across three benchmarks. The system handles videos up to 512 frames and compresses them to 128 frames for the LLM, with training reported on A100 40GB GPUs. Beyond the main scores, several analyses matter more than the topline. Convolutional fast weights consistently beat MLP-style TTT, suggesting that preserving 2D local interactions at the projector stage is useful. Causal update ordering is better than bidirectional alternatives, supporting the claim that temporal modeling should happen before compression and in the correct direction. The hybrid selector beats naive uniform sampling without requiring an extra learned scorer, which indicates that the inner-loop signals carry meaningful change information. Finally, the retention diagnostics show that fast weights are not reliable long-horizon memory. If a task requires exact evidence from far back in the video, explicit frame retention is still necessary. That boundary is one of the most useful takeaways in the paper.32:T44f,The paper formulates text generation as a finite-horizon MDP. The state is the prompt plus generated tokens, the action is the next token, the transition deterministically appends the token, and the reward is assigned by a verifier over the completed sequence. This makes RLVR different from many classical RL settings: there is little environment dynamics, but the horizon can be extremely long and rewards are often sequence-level. The analysis focuses on KL-constrained policy optimization as probability redistribution relative to a base reference model. To manipulate the initial distribution, the authors use SFT+ to increase the probability of a target string and SFT- to disrupt or suppress that behavior. They then run toy RLVR tasks, including target-string generation and math solving, under sparse, dense, and random reward signals. They also vary the prompt distribution, comparing narrow reasoning prompts against a broader Tulu-3-style pretraining distribution, and compare different model scales. Entropy and probability mass assigned to target behaviors are tracked throughout training.33:T90f,TailSFT 的核心很简单：在 SFT 时做 sequence-level filtering，把那些相对于初始策略已经“fit 得够好”的训练序列过滤掉，只对 under-modeled tail 继续更新。作者的出发点是，标准 cross-entropy 会继续奖励所有 demonstration 的概率提升，包括模型已经很容易采样出来的响应；而概率质量守恒意味着，这种继续加压可能把质量从别的潜在有用响应上挤走，导致 coverage 下降。也就是说，CE 和 coverage 不是同一个目标，甚至可能冲突。

论文先在理论上 formalize 了 coverage。设任务有二值可验证奖励，RL 初始阶段真正有用的是：对一个 prompt，模型在 k 次采样内至少出现一个 reward-bearing response 的概率，这正是 pass@k 的含义。large-k pass@k 越高，RL rollout 越容易拿到正信号。TailSFT 因而不再试图均匀提升所有监督样本的 likelihood，而是优先学习那些初始模型还不容易覆盖到的序列。

关键设计点有两个。第一，过滤是 sequence-level，而不是 token-level reweighting。因为 coverage 关心的是“完整响应是否可被采到”，不是局部 token 拟合。第二，过滤标准是相对于 initial policy，而不是相对于当前训练中的 policy。作者强调这一点并给出理论说明：若按当前策略动态过滤，训练过程会不断改变“谁算已经学会”，容易引入不稳定或错误的偏置；相对于初始策略做过滤，则更接近“保留初始模型已有覆盖、把增量学习预算投向尾部”的目标，并且能在文中的设定下给出优于标准 CE 和一些朴素过滤方案的 coverage 保证。

从算法形态看，TailSFT 是一个 drop-in SFT objective modification，不需要改模型结构，也不依赖 RL 阶段信息。实现上更像在 SFT 数据上做基于 base policy 的 hard example selection，但它不是为了提升训练时 loss 或 pass@1，而是为了提升后续 RL 可利用的探索起点。论文还提出了一个 coverage-ratio diagnostic，用 base model 和标准 SFT run 的统计量来判断 TailSFT 是否有望改善 coverage。这个诊断给了方法一个实用入口：不是所有数据集、所有任务都一定适合过滤，先看 coverage ratio 可以减少盲试。34:T85d,TailSFT is a sequence-level filtering method for supervised fine-tuning. During SFT, it removes sequences that are already sufficiently well fit by the initial policy, and concentrates updates on the under-modeled tail of the response distribution. The motivation is that standard cross-entropy keeps rewarding likelihood increases for every demonstration, including responses the model can already sample easily. Because probability mass is limited, pushing those easy responses further can reduce mass assigned to other potentially useful responses, hurting coverage.

The paper formalizes coverage in the setting of binary verifiable rewards. For a prompt x, what matters for RL initialization is the probability that at least one of k samples receives reward 1. This is exactly what pass@k measures. Large-k pass@k is therefore a proxy for how much positive learning signal RL can expect from the initialization under a realistic rollout budget.

Two design choices are central. First, filtering is done at the sequence level rather than the token level, because coverage is about sampling complete reward-bearing responses, not about local token fit. Second, filtering is defined relative to the initial policy rather than the current policy during training. This is not a cosmetic detail. The authors argue, and partially prove, that filtering relative to the initial policy better preserves useful pre-existing coverage and avoids the instability or myopia of more naive dynamic filtering schemes. Under their setup, this criterion yields better coverage guarantees than standard CE and simpler alternatives.

Operationally, TailSFT is a drop-in modification to SFT rather than a new architecture or an RL-dependent method. It resembles hard-example selection based on the base model, but the target is not better training loss or pass@1; it is better downstream RL initialization. The paper also introduces a lightweight coverage-ratio diagnostic, computed from the base model and a standard SFT run, which provides a sufficient condition for TailSFT to improve coverage. That diagnostic makes the method more actionable in practice.35:T5c1,The experiments are organized in three layers. First, the paper uses a controlled graph-navigation task to isolate the tension between CE and coverage. In that setting, filtering already-fit examples improves large-k pass@k even when cross-entropy becomes worse and pass@1 does not improve. This is an important result because it shows TailSFT is not just an empirical trick on benchmark tasks; it is optimizing a genuinely different objective.

Second, the method is tested on OLMo-3 7B for math and coding. According to the reported headline numbers, TailSFT improves pass@16 by up to 16.8 absolute points on coding and 3.1 points on math. More importantly, the gain transfers through RL: after GRPO, final pass@1 improves by up to 3.9 points. That is the strongest evidence in the paper, because it shows the method improves not only multi-sample accessibility but also the eventual post-RL model.

Third, the paper includes design ablations and diagnostics. It compares different filtering criteria and motivates why filtering relative to the initial policy is preferable to more naive alternatives. It also introduces the coverage-ratio diagnostic and shows that it can predict when TailSFT should help. A recurring theme in the results is the trade-off: TailSFT may sacrifice CE and small-k metrics in exchange for better large-k coverage and better post-RL performance. The paper treats this trade-off as a feature of stage-aware optimization rather than a weakness.36:T4ee,This one deserves a close read. Start with the introduction and the coverage formalization section. The paper only makes sense if you internalize the distinction between optimizing SFT loss and optimizing RL initialization quality. Then read the controlled graph-navigation section carefully; it gives the cleanest evidence that lower CE does not necessarily imply better downstream RL readiness. After that, focus on the TailSFT algorithm and the argument for filtering relative to the initial policy. The main empirical tables on OLMo-3 7B are the next priority, especially the pass@16 numbers and the final GRPO pass@1 gains. Finish with the coverage-ratio diagnostic, which is the part most likely to transfer directly into real training pipelines.

If time is limited, the minimum useful subset is: the coverage formalization, Algorithm 1, the graph-navigation figures, the main math/coding result tables, and the diagnostic figure. Good follow-up questions while reading are: whether the same idea extends to other stage boundaries such as pretrain-to-mid-train or mid-train-to-SFT; how filtering thresholds trade off against data diversity; and how the notion of coverage should change for multi-answer tasks, non-binary rewards, or process-based rewards.37:T7b6,文章的组织方式比一般 survey 更像问题剖析。它先把 OPSD 放回方法谱系里：SFT 提供 dense 但 off-policy 的信号；RL/RLVR 变成 on-policy，但奖励稀疏；on-policy distillation 用外部 teacher 恢复 token-level dense signal；OPSD/SDPO 则把 teacher 换成“带 privileged information 的自己”。这条脉络讲得很清楚，也解释了 OPSD 为什么同时继承了 imitation learning、RL 和 privileged learning 的优点与缺点。

全文的主线是“一个症状，三个杠杆”。从给出的内容看，这个症状主要指 OPSD 中最突出的失败模式：dense self-distillation 信号会把模型过快拉向 teacher 条件分布，导致多样性下降、探索受限、甚至学到依赖特权信息的行为。围绕这个症状，文章把控制手段归纳为三类杠杆：
1）where to apply the signal：信号施加在什么 token、什么阶段、什么轨迹片段上；
2）what to show the teacher：teacher 拿到哪类 privileged information，参考解、hint、plan、环境反馈各自引入什么偏差；
3）when to let the guidance change：训练过程中 teacher guidance 是固定、退火、门控还是分阶段切换。

文章还专门强调 KL 方向的差异。它回顾了 forward KL 与 reverse KL 在 on-policy distillation 中的不同作用：forward KL 倾向覆盖 teacher 的多模态分布，reverse KL 更容易收缩到单一模式。MiniLLM 一类工作推动了 reverse KL 在 LLM 上的使用，但在 OPSD 场景里，这种 mode-seeking 倾向会进一步放大熵塌缩问题。这个讨论对理解为什么有些 OPSD 训练“看起来收敛很快，但泛化和探索变差”很关键。

从方法论上，这篇综述最有用的地方不是罗列论文，而是把很多后续变体放进统一因果框架：它们大多是在调节 privileged teacher 与 on-policy student 之间的信息落差，以及由此产生的偏置-方差-探索三角关系。38:T86b,The paper is organized as a diagnosis rather than a catalog. It first places OPSD in a lineage: SFT gives dense but off-policy supervision; RL and RLVR move to on-policy learning but with sparse end-of-trajectory rewards; on-policy distillation restores dense token-level supervision using an external teacher; OPSD and SDPO replace that teacher with the same model under privileged conditioning. This framing is one of the paper’s strengths because it makes clear which advantages and liabilities OPSD inherits from imitation learning, RL, and learning with privileged information.

The main analytical frame is the title’s “one symptom, three levers.” The dominant symptom is that dense self-distillation can over-concentrate the student toward the privileged teacher distribution, reducing diversity and encouraging reliance on information unavailable at inference time. The three levers are then organized as:
1) where to apply the signal — which tokens, trajectory segments, or training phases should receive distillation pressure;
2) what to show the teacher — what kind of privileged information is exposed, such as reference solutions, plans, hints, or environment feedback, and what bias each introduces;
3) when to let the guidance change — whether teacher guidance should be fixed, annealed, gated, or staged over training.

A particularly useful part of the review is its treatment of KL direction. It revisits the difference between forward KL and reverse KL in on-policy distillation: forward KL encourages mode covering, while reverse KL is more mode seeking. In LLM distillation, reverse KL became popular partly through MiniLLM-style formulations, but in OPSD that same tendency can amplify entropy collapse. This helps explain why some self-distillation runs look sample-efficient and stable early on, yet end up with poorer exploration and weaker generalization.

The paper’s real contribution is conceptual unification. Many later variants are shown to be different ways of managing the information gap between privileged teacher and on-policy student, and the resulting trade-off among bias, variance, and exploration.39:T64f,As a critical review, it does not present a new unified benchmark suite. Its empirical value lies in synthesizing what the literature has already established and where disagreement remains. The paper notes that early OPSD results in mathematical reasoning were promising: accuracy could approach RL-style training while using far fewer generated tokens. That promise explains the rapid growth of the area.

At the same time, the review argues that several failure modes are now well documented:
1) entropy and diversity collapse, where dense teacher supervision narrows the student’s output distribution;
2) capability regression, where previously acquired abilities degrade under repeated self-distillation, especially when the target style becomes too tied to reference solutions;
3) dependence on privileged information, where the student is implicitly trained against token-level distributions shaped by information unavailable at test time;
4) limited strategy discovery, where OPSD is better at sharpening reasoning patterns already present in the base model than at discovering genuinely new ones, in contrast to some RLVR settings.

The paper is careful about what is settled and what is still implementation-dependent. Outcomes vary with KL choice, the type of privileged information, whether guidance is annealed, and whether distillation is applied selectively to uncertain or erroneous tokens. If the practical question is whether OPSD can replace RL, the review’s answer is cautious: it offers dense supervision and token efficiency, but its bias is not something that disappears with minor tuning.3a:T793,The analysis targets the Thinker backbone of Qwen3-Omni-30B-A3B-Instruct: 48 layers, hidden size 2048, with audio encoder features injected at audio-token positions before the text Transformer continues processing. The authors do not train probes or modify the model. They apply a logit lens to intermediate residual states, projecting hidden states through the final untied output head and measuring the rank of target tokens in vocabulary space. They use token rank rather than raw probability because logit scale drifts substantially across depth.

A key design choice is to predefine the workspace band as layers 17-38, roughly 35%-80% of depth, following prior text-model workspace studies instead of tuning the band on audio data. Readout is taken at audio-token positions, and for each answer option they report the best rank achieved by its first token across those positions and layers.

The central control isolates audio evidence from text prior. The written question and options are kept byte-identical, while only the waveform is swapped among the true clip, a mismatched clip from the same category, or silence. Because audio features are injected at positions that precede the textual question/options in the causal stream, readouts at those positions cannot simply copy the later option text. This makes a strong case that readable option-related concepts in the middle layers are driven by the waveform.

The paper then studies five aspects: hidden multi-hop concept formation, multilinguality of the workspace, differences between direct audio input and text caption input, depth-wise emergence of audio-driven signals, and layer-deletion evidence about where listening, integration, and output delivery happen. The resulting picture is a three-band organization: early sensory access, middle verbalizable workspace, and late output alignment. Errors often appear early as text-mode priors and are rarely corrected later.3b:T8f2,The experiments are mainly controlled interpretability studies rather than benchmark-style evaluation. The model under study is Qwen3-Omni-30B-A3B-Instruct with a 48-layer Thinker backbone.

Five empirical findings stand out.

1. Hidden reasoning can be read without chain-of-thought. In the most striking example, for an audio clip whose verbatim transcription is essentially garbled, the middle-layer readout progresses through “water + gate,” then “scandal,” then “president,” and finally resolves to “Nixon.” This suggests that latent multi-hop semantic structure is already present before the model emits any answer.

2. The workspace is multilingual. On entirely English inputs, 38.5% of top-1 readouts are Chinese. The implication is that the intermediate workspace is not tied to the surface language of the prompt, but behaves more like a shared semantic space with multiple lexicalizations.

3. Audio preserves information that text captions lose. The authors compare direct audio input against feeding the model its own emotion-stripped caption of the same clip. The audio pathway recovers sound source, speaker role, and affective information that the text pathway does not. This supports the claim that the readable middle-layer content is not reducible to transcript text.

4. Audio-driven signals emerge at a specific depth range. The gap over text prior is near zero at input, becomes significant from roughly 12% depth onward, and is most cleanly separated from prior in the predefined workspace band (layers 17-38). The paper also argues that these middle-layer states are causally used before the final motor layers.

5. The answer is distributed, and hallucinations are born early. Layer deletion localizes “listening” to early entry layers and “delivery” to the final output layer, while no single interior layer is indispensable. A text-mode hallucination often takes over early and is usually not revised later.

The authors are careful about limitations: they use a logit lens rather than the more faithful Jacobian lens; they study one model family; the dataset slice is small and intentionally sound-dominated; and readout is sparse across audio positions. So the results should be read as mechanism evidence, not as a capability benchmark.3c:T4cb,For a quick but useful read, focus on three parts.

First, read the introduction and method carefully to understand the control logic. The most important point is how the paper separates waveform-driven internal content from prompt prior: byte-identical text, waveform-only swaps, and readout at audio-token positions that causally precede the options.

Second, inspect the qualitative case studies tied to the five findings, especially the Watergate/Nixon example, the multilingual readouts, and the audio-versus-caption comparisons. These examples carry most of the paper’s interpretability argument.

Third, read the depth analysis and layer-deletion sections. That is where the paper becomes relevant to pretraining researchers: it suggests that audio information enters a shared language-like workspace relatively early, and that hallucinations may crystallize before late layers have any chance to repair them.

A 30-45 minute skim is enough to extract the main ideas. If reading more deeply, the two questions worth pushing on are: how faithful the logit-lens readout is in the audio setting, and whether this middle-layer verbal workspace is specific to Qwen3-Omni or a broader property of multimodal language models.3d:T46d,The pipeline has three stages. First, captions are generated on 200 COCO val2014 images, and object mentions are labeled as grounded or hallucinated using a COCO-based CHAIR scorer. For each object token, the method records image-token attention for all 1,024 attention heads in LLaVA-1.5-7B and ranks heads by the drop in image attention around hallucinated object words relative to grounded ones. Second, the strongest candidates are screened by single-head ablation, measuring the change in hallucination-token log probability. This yields a fixed set of 32 heads spanning 19 transformer layers. Third, two interventions are applied only to this set: a head-sliced LoRA adapter on the corresponding Q/K/V head dimensions, enforced with gradient masking, and an inference-time grounding controller that penalizes generation when the selected heads attend weakly to image tokens. The LoRA stage uses a DPO-style objective where COCO ground-truth captions are chosen responses and the model’s hallucinated captions are rejected responses. The reported LoRA configuration is rank 8, alpha 16, dropout 0.05, trained for three epochs.3e:T517,MHS is built around a model-agnostic standardized hardware driver. The driver translates device-specific interfaces into simple primitives such as read, for retrieving temperature, images, curves, or other state, and write, for setting temperature, positions, laser parameters, or other controllable values. Devices are made discoverable in a standard format so that agents and instruments can find and communicate with each other across networks without custom translators. A second important design is the use of natural-language tags. Many hardware-relevant facts are not visible from code alone, such as the weight of a robot arm, operating limits, what the device can measure, and what can be safely adjusted. MHS lets users enter this information directly, or have an agent interview them about the setup. The driver then produces a reference file describing the device’s capabilities and enforced safety limits. Control is exposed through MCP, command-line interfaces, and code-file APIs. Online model reasoning is used for high-level supervision, monitoring, fault handling, and parameter updates, while long-running or fast operations can be compiled into deterministic scripts. This separation is important because physical control often needs low latency, reproducibility, and auditability.3f:T4e0,The blog reports a research preview and partner deployments rather than a controlled benchmark with ablations. The reported integration claim is that MHS can reduce setup work from weeks or months to hours or minutes. Carnegie Mellon used MHS for serial dilution dose-response experiments and reports roughly a 3x speedup, with an agent orchestrating a liquid handler, plate reader, robotic arm, and monitoring cameras across three computers with incompatible interfaces. QuEra used MHS to give an agent control over parts of the laser system in neutral-atom quantum machines; the agent-developed controller recovered laser lock 99.3% of the time without human intervention. Other deployments include Genentech automating a BCA protein assay across a liquid handler, robotic arm, and plate reader; University of Washington work on remote monitoring, agent-supervised qPCR, and collision-free plate handoffs; HHMI Janelia unifying a microscopy rig that previously depended on seven vendor programs; and Tetsuwan integrating MHS with ResearchOS for a qPCR workflow. The evidence is promising but incomplete: there is no public standardized task suite, failure taxonomy, model comparison, or detailed safety incident analysis in the provided material.15:["$","main",null,{"className":"flex-1 overflow-y-auto","children":["$","div",null,{"className":"mx-auto max-w-4xl px-4 py-8 sm:px-6 xl:max-w-6xl","children":["$","$L16",null,{"data":{"date":"2026-08-28","generated_at":"2026-08-28T01:47:03.475632+00:00","stats":{"total":100,"starred":5,"must_read":8,"skim":44,"glance":48,"by_category":{"pretraining_data":3,"industry":3,"llm_pretrain":2,"scaling":1,"architecture":4,"efficient":14,"eval":15,"multimodal":8,"multimodal_gen":4,"post_train":16,"mechanism":10,"coding_agent":7,"agent":13},"fetch_status":{"arxiv":"ok","huggingface":"ok","blog":"ok","twitter":"empty","github_trending":"ok","hf_trending":"ok"}},"categories":[{"id":"pretraining_data","emoji":"📚","name":"预训练数据","description":"以预训练数据为核心贡献的工作：数据采集/清洗/去重、数据配比(data mixture)、数据筛选/质量评估、语料构建、数据课程(data curriculum)、数据重复(repetition)、合成数据用于预训练、Tokenizer/词表设计、成员推断/数据检测。判据是『论文核心是数据本身』，而非训练 recipe/优化器(→ llm_pretrain)或评测资产(→ eval)","papers":[{"id":"2608.25826","title":"Unfolding Scientific Papers into Multi-Turn Generation Trajectories for Continued Pre-Training","abstract":"A recent line of synthetic-data work reconstructs the thinking behind existing text rather than rewriting the text itself, but it operates on short web passages, recovers only local thoughts, and leaves the structure of whole documents untouched. correlation衡量一组变量在给定历史条件下相互依赖的总量。这个恒等式给并行解码的轮数下界和schedule设计提供了统一账本：如果条件依赖可以通过递归结构快速消除，就能用少量轮数；如果依赖在许多分支上持续存在，再强的自适应策略也需要更多串行深度。","technical_approach":"设目标是一个离散向量的联合分布。每轮policy根据已揭示坐标和值，确定下一批未揭示坐标；这些坐标不是从真实联合条件分布采样，而是分别从各自的精确条件边缘分布独立采样。误差用forward KL衡量，因为它对应额外期望log loss，并且能沿reveal过程做chain-rule分解。作者定义serial depth为在目标分布平均意义下达到给定KL预算所需的最小轮数。核心技术是KL链式分解与conditional total correlation的对齐：每一轮的误差正好是该轮所揭示坐标在当前历史条件下的total correlation，整体误差是这些项对policy随机路径的期望和。这个结果覆盖随机大小、历史依赖、由生成值决定的reveal sets。基于这个恒等式，作者分析多类分布：Bernoulli walk可用层级二分以对数轮数精确采样，而连续左到右分块schedule在固定误差下需要线性轮数；有限阶Markov链存在零误差schedule，轮数与Markov order成正比、与序列长度成对数关系；均匀随机排列在固定误差预算下需要线性期望轮数，并且固定轮数下的最优误差可化为整数composition问题；二元字母表上，balanced binary strings达到log^2 n量级深度，independent one-hot blocks给出对任意确定性value-adaptive policy的sqrt深度下界，矩形block构造能实现最高到1/2的多项式指数。","experiments":"理论实验给出多个清晰的深度分离。Bernoulli walk展示了递归条件独立结构的好处：hierarchical bisection可在O(log n)轮内精确采样；但连续左到右schedule即使允许固定KL误差也需要线性轮数。随机排列展示相反情形：坐标间的全局排斥依赖很难被并行边缘采样保留，固定误差下需要线性期望轮数；在hard round cap下，最优近似误差被精确表述为整数composition问题，并进一步推出固定轮数渐近、有限block的pairs-first阶段和联合scaling边界。二元分布族说明小词表并不自动意味着低串行深度：uniform balanced binary strings在固定正误差预算下为Θ((log n)^2)，one-hot block构造给出匹配的平方根型困难例子，矩形block覆盖从polylog到n^{1/2}之间的多项式深度。经验部分使用一个0.5B模型和单一采样温度，在WikiText与四个领域的model-generated text上取542条序列，诊断generic schedules和实际解码规则；另有231个held-out prompts的paired self-sampling实验，用外部自回归评估器连接teacher-forced pseudo-cost与输出质量，并测试一种模型无关的spaced-profile schedule。作者明确把这些实验定位为诊断，而不是通用benchmark。","reading_guide":"精读应先看问题定义和oracle model：每轮独立采样精确条件边缘，这个设定把模型预测误差剥离，只保留并行化带来的分布偏差。随后重点看KL与conditional total correlation的恒等式证明，这是全篇的主轴。Bernoulli walk一节值得细读，因为它清楚展示了为什么层级揭示比连续分块更适合有递归结构的分布。随机排列一节适合关注下界技术和hard-cap最优误差刻画的读者。二元构造部分说明serial depth不是由alphabet size、entropy或NLL单独决定，适合和masked diffusion LM的confidence schedule联系起来读。实验部分建议快速读设置和主要图表，重点看teacher-forced pseudo-cost是否能预测自采样质量，以及spaced-profile schedule为何能作为简单强基线。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper studies the serial cost of adaptive parallel sampling for discrete vectors, motivated by masked language models and masked diffusion decoders. In each round, a policy observes previously revealed values, chooses a set of unrevealed coordinates, and samples those coordinates independently from their exact conditional marginals. The question is how many sequential rounds are necessary to keep the forward KL from the target joint distribution below a prescribed budget. The hard part is value adaptivity: the reveal sets are random, history-dependent, and coupled to the sampled values.","importance":"The main contribution is a clean information accounting principle for parallel masked decoding. The forward KL introduced by a deterministic value-adaptive policy is exactly the expected conditional total correlation accumulated over its reveal rounds. Conditional total correlation measures the dependence among the coordinates sampled in the same round after conditioning on the current history. This gives a precise answer to what parallel decoding discards: not entropy, not negative log-likelihood by itself, but within-round conditional dependence. For pretraining researchers working on masked diffusion LMs or any-order LMs, this result clarifies when parallel generation is structurally cheap and when it requires many serial refinement steps even with perfect conditional marginals.","technical_approach":"$18","experiments":"$19","reading_guide":"Read the formal model first, especially the distinction between exact conditional marginals and the true joint conditional over a reveal batch. The Bernoulli walk section is the clearest example of why recursive conditional-independence structure enables logarithmic depth. The random permutation section is the best place to understand lower-bound techniques and the hard-round-cap characterization. The binary-alphabet constructions are useful for readers asking whether small vocabularies make parallel decoding easy; they do not. This makes traditional self-consistency methods (like majority voting) ineffective, as consistent incorrect answers can mask true reasoning signals.","importance":"This paper introduces PDC (Prefix-Denoising Consistency), a test-time verification method for DLMs. By using prefix-conditioned regeneration to break trajectory homogeneity under low-temperature decoding, it provides an effective and compute-efficient strategy for test-time scaling in DLMs.","technical_approach":"PDC exploits a characteristic of DLMs: initially correct trajectories are more stable and reproducible than incorrect ones. Once reasoning traces reach tens of thousands of tokens, keeping every intermediate token becomes expensive, while many of those tokens no longer contribute much to the next step.","importance":"The main relevance is the interface between pretraining, inference-time scaling, and RL post-training. Long RLVR rollouts and reasoning-oriented sampling often fail because overlong generations are truncated or discarded. Prefix Sliding offers a training-free approximation: keep the instruction prefix and a recent window, and evict stale intermediate reasoning tokens. This changes the memory bound from growing with the full trace length to depending only on the prefix size and window length, making 100k-token rollouts more practical.","technical_approach":"Prefix Sliding keeps two regions in attention memory: a fixed prefix and a sliding window of the most recent generated tokens. The prefix contains system instructions, task prompts, tool descriptions, and markers such as <think>; it also serves as an attention sink. The recent window carries the active reasoning state. Older intermediate reasoning tokens are removed from the KV-cache as generation proceeds. For positional embeddings such as RoPE, the paper discusses Reset PE and Continue PE. Continue PE reuses cached representations and avoids reapplying positional embeddings, and the reported appendix results suggest little performance difference, so it is the default. For RL training, the paper proposes chunked backpropagation and truncated backpropagation. Chunked backprop computes gradients over chunks and accumulates them. Truncated backprop only computes loss on the final chunk, using preceding tokens as context.","experiments":"The provided content reports that Prefix Sliding can match full-attention performance while running about 3× faster without additional training, and that it enables RL rollouts beyond 100,000 tokens. One concrete example uses a sliding window of 2048 for a 100,000-token reasoning trace. The sampler sends only the last 8192 tokens to the trainer; the first 6144 tokens are used as context, and token-level RL loss is applied only to the final 2048 tokens. The paper notes that the theoretical receptive field of a W-window, L-layer stack is W×L, but the effective receptive field is closer to 1.5×W in practice because of bottlenecks. Appendix results are said to show small differences between Continue PE and Reset PE, and comparable performance between truncated backpropagation and full-attention full backpropagation, although detailed tables are not included in the provided excerpt.","reading_guide":"A skim read of 20–30 minutes is enough. enforcement实验。ContourKV本身可以快速扫过，重点不是新规则胜出，而是它作为受控对照验证了selection上限很低。附录A的NP-complete证明适合需要理论边界的读者细读。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper studies a specific failure mode in KV-cache eviction: deployed operators keep a subset of keys, renormalize the original attention weights over that subset, and therefore operate under forced weights. The central question is how much subset selection can still improve the approximation under this constraint, and whether reported gaps between eviction methods actually come from selection quality, memory accounting, query visibility, or extra computation.","importance":"The useful lesson for long-context inference research is that KV eviction results need byte-level accounting. The paper shows that keeping the largest attention masses is already close to the best possible subset under forced weights. If this measurement holds, increasingly elaborate query-agnostic selectors have little headroom unless they change the operator, use query information, or exploit implementation details. This matters for pretraining-oriented long-context work as well, because model and data decisions should be separated from artifacts introduced by non-shrinking cache masks or unfair budget enforcement.","technical_approach":"The analysis models one attention row as a probability vector p over value vectors v_j. Dense attention returns μ, while a kept set A returns m_A, the original weights restricted to A and renormalized. The forced-weight error is es(A)=||μ-m_A||. This is contrasted with a free-weight geometric error ef(A), where weights on the retained points may be re-solved. The paper shows that these two objectives can differ in both value and minimizer. It also proves that deciding whether the forced-weight optimum ES(s) is zero is NP-complete even in a one-dimensional uniform-mass integer setting with s=N/2. Since the exact problem is combinatorial, the authors enumerate restricted candidates and use a cheap balancing selector that starts from the top-mass set and greedily swaps retained and dropped keys when the swap reduces es(A). This selector reads the dense output μ, so it is mainly a diagnostic and decode-time tool rather than a query-agnostic cache precompression rule.","experiments":"The main measurement enumerates exact best subsets on 168,192 attention rows from five models. The best subset improves over top-mass by only a median 2% to 5% of the remaining gap to full attention. The paper then audits established eviction methods in a shared evaluation pipeline. Several strong query-agnostic methods nominally compress the cache, but their per-head selections are stored as masks over a full non-shrinking cache; true memory savings require ragged per-head storage. Enforcing the nominal budget on fixed selections costs 14.1 to 62.2 benchmark points, and an 87.6-point retrieval gap is traced to rankings computed after the question is visible. The proposed ContourKV rule is training-free, reuses existing importance scores, and physically enforces the budget before evaluation. It wins 93 of 160 comparisons against KVzip and loses 22; at matched memory it ties Compactor, supporting the claim that near-optimal forced-weight selectors should not separate much by selection alone.","reading_guide":"A 20–30 minute skim is enough for most readers. Start with the Introduction for the decomposition of reported gains into selection, memory, query information, and compute. Then read the formal setup in Operator and Frozen-Head Measurements, especially the distinction between forced and free weights. Readers working on inference systems should focus on the memory-accounting experiments and budget enforcement results in Section 4. ContourKV is best read as a controlled probe rather than as the main contribution. Appendix A is useful if the computational hardness of the forced-weight subset problem matters for follow-up work.","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"这篇会改变对 KV eviction 论文的信任基线；先看最优子集枚举和 bytes accounting，很多 margin 可能是评测假象。","en":"This should reset your trust baseline for KV-eviction papers; read the optimal-subset enumeration and byte accounting before believing reported margins."},"metadata":{"institutions":["Stanford University","Affiliation:Stanford University"],"authors":["Jack Shi","Jerry Gu"],"notable_authors":[],"tags":["KV-cache","long-context","serving","attention","evaluation"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25230","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.25230","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2608.24938","title":"ExFold: Unified Expert Folding for Training-Free MoE Prefill-Decode Acceleration","abstract":"Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models scale capacity for strong quality while keeping per-token compute bounded through sparse expert activation. Yet low-latency MoE serving is increasingly challenging, because it spans two inference phases with fundamentally different bottlenecks: prefill is dominated by token-wise expert computation, whereas decode is constrained by memory traffic from the batch-wise activated expert set. However, existing training-free acceleration methods optimize only a single re","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-08-24T08:53:58Z","category_id":"efficient","cover_url":"/covers/2608_24938.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.AI"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作处理的是 MoE 推理里一个很具体但长期被拆开处理的问题：prefill 和 decode 的瓶颈不同，前者主要受每个 token 实际执行了多少个 expert 影响，后者主要受一个 batch 内被激活的 expert 并集大小影响；现有 training-free 加速方法通常只优化其中一个资源代理，却没有认真处理“被裁掉的 expert 贡献如何补回”这个误差源。论文要解决的不是单纯少跑几个 expert，而是在不同阶段、不同预算约束下，用统一机制近似恢复被排除 expert 的输出贡献，把 MoE 加速从资源裁剪问题改写成输出重构问题。","importance":"对基座研究的直接意义不在预训练本身，而在 MoE 架构落地与 serving 约束的建模方式。它给出的启发有两点。第一，MoE 推理优化不能只看 top-k 稀疏路由本身，还要看被删掉的 expert mass 如何显式近似，否则预算一紧，误差会快速累积。第二，prefill/decode 应该被视为两种不同的约束优化问题，但可以共享同一套误差补偿算子。这种“阶段感知 + 统一近似器”的思路，对后续做 MoE serving、动态路由压缩、甚至训练期蒸馏式路由校正都有参考价值。贡献量级属于工程方法上比较扎实的一类：不是改模型训练范式，但对大规模 MoE 部署有现实意义。","technical_approach":"方法核心叫 Expert Folding。出发点是：如果某个 source expert 因预算限制不执行，不能简单丢掉，也不只是把 token 改路由到一个相似 expert，而是把 source expert 的贡献投影到一个被保留的 target expert 上。作者先做了两个经验观察：一是很多 expert 对同一输入会产生方向上相近的输出，说明存在可替代性；二是不同 expert 输出幅值差异很大，说明只做相似 expert 替换会有明显尺度失配。基于这两点，方法为每层离线校准两个矩阵。其一是 scalar-projector matrix，给出 source→target 的有向标量投影系数，用来修正尺度；其二是 projection-loss matrix，衡量把某个 source expert 折叠到某个 target expert 时的重构损失。校准过程只需要在无标注文本上做一次冻结前向，不需要再训练模型。推理时，先按阶段预算选保留 expert：prefill 为每个 token 选 K_pre 个主导 expert，decode 为整个 batch 选 D 个主导 expert。对于未保留的 expert，利用 loss matrix 找到最小损失 target，再把对应 scalar 吸收到 target 的 router weight 中，相当于把被删 expert 的门控质量折叠到保留 expert 上。这样做的关键区别在于：现有 token-wise sparsification 主要减少每 token 执行 expert 数；expert-set consolidation 主要减少 batch 激活 expert 集；而 ExFold 不把两者看成两套独立方法，而是用同一个 folding operator 处理“被排除贡献的恢复”。实现上作者把它做成 vLLM 插件，并写了轻量 CUDA kernel 来执行 folding。","experiments":"实验覆盖多个 MoE 架构和真实 vLLM serving workload，重点看 TTFT、TPOT 和质量保持。论文给出的主结果是：在 training-free 条件下，TTFT 最多提升 1.41×，TPOT 最多提升 2.45×，平均质量大约保留原模型的 99%。这个结果说明方法在 decode 侧收益更大，符合其针对 batch-level expert union 的设计预期。实验结构上，值得注意的不是单个最好数字，而是几组对比。第一，和只做 pruning/skipping 的方法比，ExFold 在相同预算下质量掉得更慢，说明显式重构被删 expert 贡献确实有效。第二，和静态 merge 类方法比，它能随 token 或 batch 的实时预算变化而调整，不会把压缩误差固化进一个永久子模型。第三，和相似性 rerouting 比，加入有向标量投影后，能更好处理 expert 输出尺度不一致的问题。消融上，论文强调 scalar projector 和 loss-based target selection 都是必要部件；如果只做 nearest expert 替换而不做尺度校正，误差明显更大。另一个重要点是同一套校准矩阵可同时服务 prefill 与 decode，这降低了部署复杂度，也说明作者定义的 folding 单元具有阶段无关性。","reading_guide":"泛读时先抓三件事。第一，看 Introduction 和 Figure 1，先把 prefill 与 decode 的瓶颈差异吃透；如果这个问题设定没建立起来，后面的统一框架就容易看成普通近似推理。第二，看方法部分里关于 Figure 3 的经验观察，以及 scalar-projector matrix / projection-loss matrix 的定义，这是整篇工作的真正创新点。第三，看实验里与 pruning、merging、rerouting 的对比和消融，重点判断“为什么不是简单 reroute 就够了”。预计 25 到 35 分钟可以完成一轮有效阅读。若要深挖，建议进一步看校准矩阵如何构造、是否依赖校准语料分布、不同层 expert 可替代性是否稳定，以及 folding 后 router weight 的数值稳定性和 kernel 开销。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper addresses a specific serving problem in MoE models that is usually split into two unrelated optimizations. Prefill is dominated by per-token expert compute, while decode is dominated by the batch-level union of activated experts and the associated memory traffic. Existing training-free methods typically optimize only one proxy budget—experts per token or experts per batch—and largely ignore a harder question: what should replace the contribution of the experts that are removed? The paper reframes MoE acceleration as output reconstruction under phase-specific budgets, rather than simple expert reduction.","importance":"Its value is mainly on the systems-and-modeling boundary of MoE deployment. Two ideas matter. First, MoE acceleration quality is not determined only by how many experts are skipped, but by whether the excluded expert mass is explicitly reconstructed. Second, prefill and decode should be treated as different constrained inference regimes, yet they can share one approximation mechanism. That is a useful design pattern for future MoE serving work, dynamic routing compression, and possibly training-time routing distillation. This is not a pretraining recipe paper, but it is a solid serving-side contribution for sparse foundation models.","technical_approach":"$1a","experiments":"$1b","reading_guide":"For a quick but useful read, focus on three parts. First, read the introduction and the figure that contrasts prefill and decode bottlenecks; that framing is the foundation of the whole paper. Second, inspect the empirical observations behind Expert Folding, especially the directional alignment and magnitude mismatch story, because that is where the method departs from simple rerouting. Third, read the experimental comparisons against pruning, merging, and rerouting, plus the ablations on the scalar projector. A first pass should take about 25–35 minutes. timing 与硬件遥测对齐，能解释为什么某种量化或后端在 edge 上未必带来预期收益。第三，它给出跨 SoC 代际、跨 backend、跨 precision 的统一 schema，这对后续做 edge serving、量化策略、系统 co-design 的工作是一个比较扎实的基线工具。若从“对预训练研究有没有启发”来讲，启发主要在于：模型设计和量化格式的收益高度依赖部署环境，不能把 schema 至少覆盖 tokenization、prefill、逐 token generation、de-tokenization、ITL 和 end-to-end latency。然后再把这些阶段边界与 tegrastats、NVML 等高频硬件遥测对齐，得到 CPU、GPU、DRAM、功耗、能耗、温度等指标在 prefill 和 decode throughput、以及输入输出长度扩展，分别作用在推理流水线的不同部分。第二，量化通常能降低 DRAM 流量和模型 footprint，但不必然降低延迟或能耗，因为 edge SoC 上还受 CPU 侧调度、GPU 利用率、共享内存带宽、DVFS 和热约束影响。第三，不同后端的 CPU orchestration 成本差异明显，这会改变 decode 阶段的有效吞吐和能效。第四，跨代 SoC 拉到同一测量口径；最后看结果部分里 phase-level timing、resource utilization、energy/thermal 三组图。重点不是记住哪台 Jetson 快多少，而是看作者如何用对齐后的 telemetry 解释性能差异。预计 20 到 30 分钟能抓住主要信息。若要深挖，建议关注：1）phase 边界定义是否足够稳健；2）不同量化格式在不同 backend 上的 That makes it hard to compare SoC generations, hard to align HuggingFace and llama.cpp under one methodology, and hard to explain how prefill and decode stress the system differently.
